# UI Hooks

## Overview

UI hooks allow you to customize the visual appearance and layout of the application. These hooks enable you to add custom components to the AppBar, customize the hero section, modify the logo, and change footer text.

## AppBar Extra Components

### Purpose

Add custom components to the application's AppBar (top navigation bar), such as notification bells, user menus, theme toggles, or quick action buttons.

### Basic Implementation

```typescript
// src/custom/components/NotificationBell.tsx
import { FC } from 'react';
import { IconButton, Badge } from '@mui/material';
import { Notifications as NotificationsIcon } from '@mui/icons-material';

export const NotificationBell: FC = () => {
  const notificationCount = 4; // Replace with actual logic

  const handleClick = () => {
    // Open notifications panel
    console.log('Open notifications');
  };

  return (
    <IconButton color="inherit" onClick={handleClick}>
      <Badge badgeContent={notificationCount} color="error">
        <NotificationsIcon />
      </Badge>
    </IconButton>
  );
};
```

### Registration

The AppBar accepts exactly one extra component, registered as a Pandino service under `CUSTOM_VISUAL_ELEMENT_INTERFACE_KEY` (value `'CustomVisualElement'`, exported from `~/custom`) with the service property `component: 'AppBarExtraComponents'`. The servic

title: Frontend Extension Points

# Frontend Extension Points

Supported extension points of the generated React frontend that are not hooks of a specific page.

## Extra Routes

`src/extra-routes.tsx` exports `extraRoutes: Array<{ path: string; element: ReactElement; exact?: boolean }>`. It is generated empty, and the generated `routes.tsx` prepends these routes to the generated ones. Add custom React Router routes there instead of editing generated routing files (protect the file in `.generator-ignore`).

## Guest Page

With `supportGuestAccess` enabled on the application, an unauthenticated user is not redirected to `signinRedirect`. The auth gate renders a guest page instead. Register your own component as a service under `GUEST_PAGE_INTERFACE_KEY` (the value `'GuestPage'`, imported from `~/auth/Auth`):

```typescript
context.registerService<FC<any>>(GUEST_PAGE_INTERFACE_KEY, GuestPage);
```

The scaffold `src/custom/hooks/default-guest-page-hook-registration.tsx.default` contains `registerGuestPage(context)`. The default page shows a Login / Register button (i18n key `judo.guest-page.login-register`) that calls `signinRedirect()`.

## Sub-Theme Hooks

A named sub-theme is registered by `register<SubTheme>SubThemeHook(context)` (scaffold `src/custom/hooks/sub-themes/register<Name>SubTh
[921] TOOLRESULT: ---
title: Frontend Table Hooks
description: Table row highlighting, column customizer and sidekick components of the generated React frontend.

# Table Hooks

## Overview

**Where the names come from.** `TableRowHighlightingHook` and its key live in `~/theme/table-row-highlighting`; `ColumnCustomizerHook`, `TABLE_COLUMN_CUSTOMIZER_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` and `SidekickComponentProps` live in `~/utilities`; the sidekick key is generated per table component. The `component` service property is the generated table component name (see `data-testid`/component names under `src/containers/**/components/`). There is no `~/generated` barrel.

Table hooks allow you to customize the behavior and appearance of data tables. These hooks enable row highlighting, custom columns, sidekick components (above-table UI), and column customization.

## Table Row Highlighting

[925] TOOLRESULT: 
There is no generic `CUSTOM_COMPONENT_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` or `CustomComponentHook`. A custom implementation is a visual element of the model that the generator exposes as an extension point: register an `FC` under `CUSTOM_VISUAL_ELEMENT_INTERFACE_KEY` (from `~/custom`) with the service property `component` set to the per-element key generated in the container's `customization.ts`. The scaffold `src/custom/hooks/custom-implementations/register<Container>CustomImplementations.tsx.default` is generated for containers that have such elements and contains one `register<Interface>CustomImplementation(context)` function per element with the correct proxy props type (`GenericProxyProps`, `TableProxyProps`, `LinkProxyProps` from `~/custom`). The element is created in the ESM UI model as a custom implementation widget, not by replacing arbitrary generated components.

```typescript
// from the scaffold
export function registerMyElementCustomImplementation(context: BundleContext) {
  context.registerService<FC<GenericProxyProps>>(CUSTOM_VISUAL_ELEMENT_INTERFACE_KEY, myElementCustomImplementation, {
    component: MY_ELEMENT_COMPONENT_INTERFACE_KEY,
  });
}
```

Call the register function from `application-customizer.tsx`. The examples that follow show the content of such a component; their `actions.*` props are illustrative and the real props are those of the proxy props type in the scaffold.

[937] TOOLRESULT: 
- The generated React table is an MUI DataGrid. Row-level actions come from the model: row action definitions such as view, remove and delete (`*RowViewButton`, `*RowRemoveButton`, `*RowDeleteButton`) and custom operations placed in a table's `rowOperations` (`OperationFormTableRowCallOperationButton`). See [row actions](../../agent-docs/frontend/model-screen-layout.md) and [table operations](../../agent-docs/frontend/esm-to-ui-mappings/tables-navigation.md).
- The platform has a supported way to add or replace a single column on a generated table without replacing the table: a column customizer hook registered under `TABLE_COLUMN_CUSTOMIZER_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` with the table component name and the column as service properties. Its documented example is an action button column with `renderCell` ([table hooks](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/table-hooks.md)).
- Hooks and custom code you add under `src/custom/` are not generated, so they survive regeneration ([extension points](../../agent-docs/frontend/extension-points.md)).
[938] TOOLRESULT: 
**Interface Keys:**
- String identifiers for each customizable feature
- Example: `TABLE_ROW_HIGHLIGHTING_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY`
- Exported from the module that owns the hook (for example `~/theme/table-row-highlighting`, `~/layout/Footer`) or from the generated `~/containers/<path>/customization` module for container hooks; there is no `~/generated` barrel

**Service Properties:**
- Configuration objects: `{ component, column, page }`
- Filter which components the hook applies to
- Example: `{ component: 'ServiceCampaignForm', column: 'name' }`

**Hook Implementation:**
- Must be React hooks for DI support
- Follow TypeScript interfaces
- Return functions or configuration objects

**Precedence:**
- Page-level hooks override container-level hooks
- More specific hooks override general hooks

Part of a page needs UI the generator cannot produce (charts, masked inputs, rich viewers). Model that part as a custom implementation element and supply the React component yourself; the rest of the page stays generated.

[945] TOOLRESULT: 
- A custom implementation is a visual element of the ESM UI model that the generator exposes as an extension point. It is not a way to swap an arbitrary generated component: the element must be modeled first ([widgets: placeholder / custom implementation](../../agent-docs/frontend/esm-to-ui-mappings/widgets.md), [advanced patterns: custom component replacement](../../agent-docs/frontend/advanced-patterns.md)).
- You register a function component as a Pandino service under `CUSTOM_VISUAL_ELEMENT_INTERFACE_KEY` (exported from `~/custom`) with the service property `component` set to the per-element key generated in the container's `customization.ts`. There is no generic `CUSTOM_COMPONENT_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY`.
- For containers that have such elements the generator creates the scaffold `src/custom/hooks/custom-implementations/register<Container>CustomImplementations.tsx.default` with one `register<Interface>CustomImplementation(context)` function per element and the right props type (`GenericProxyProps`, `TableProxyProps`, `LinkProxyProps` from `~/custom`). Copy it without `.default`, implement the component and call the register function from `application-customizer.tsx` ([hooks: using .default scaffolds](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/README.md)).
- The same service key also carries other custom elements, such as table sidekicks and the AppBar extra component ([table hooks](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/table-hooks.md), [UI hooks](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/ui-hooks.md))
[946] TOOLRESULT: 
- **Model first**: a string DataField with `textWidget=INPUT`, `textMultiLine=false` and a `textMask` pattern needs no custom code. Use the steps below only when that is not enough.
- **Registration**: the element must be exposed by the model as a custom implementation. Register an FC with `registerService(CUSTOM_VISUAL_ELEMENT_INTERFACE_KEY, ..., { component: <ELEMENT>_COMPONENT_INTERFACE_KEY })` using the per-element key generated in the container's `customization.ts`; the scaffold `register<Container>CustomImplementations.tsx.default` has the function. Call it from `application-customizer.tsx`. rackinspect example key: `SERVICES_PARTNER_SERVICE_PARTNER_PARTNER_VIEW_EDIT_VATS_GROUP_COMPONENT`.
- **Component shape**: A React FC receiving `GenericProxyProps` (rackinspect version: `GenericProxyProps<TStored, TActionDefs>`) with `data`, `validation`, `editMode`, `storeDiff`, `isLoading`, `actions`
- **Masked input**: MUI TextField with `InputProps.inputComponent: IMaskInput as any` and `inputProps` containing mask definition (`mask: '00000000-0-00'`), `placeholderChar: '_'`, `lazy: false`, `overwrite: true`
- **Value handling**: Uses `onAccept` callback (not `onChange`) to capture the unmasked value, with null handling for empty/placeholder values
- **Uppercase normalization**: Optionally applies `String(value).toUpperCase()` on accepted values
- **Validation**: Reads from the validation Map via `validation.get('fieldName')` and displays as helperText
- **Disabled/readonly**:

[948] TOOLRESULT: 
**Supertypes:** `Widget` → `Component` → `FormElement` → `VisualElement` → `NamedElement`

Reserved space for custom implementations or future content.

| ESM Element | ESM Attribute | UI Model | Frontend Effect |
|-------------|---------------|----------|-----------------|
| `ui:Placeholder` | `name` | Placeholder name | Component name |
| `ui:Placeholder` | `col` | Grid column | Layout width |
| `ui:Placeholder` | `row` | Row span | Layout height |
| `ui:Placeholder` | `customImplementation` | Custom flag | Marked for custom implementation |

**Example:**
```xml
<components xsi:type="ui:Placeholder"
  xmi:id="_PH123"
  name="customSection"
  customImplementation="true"
  col="12"/>
```

- Files you add in `src/custom/` are not generated, so they need no `.generator-ignore` entry (the folder is committed; only `.default` scaffolds are gitignored) ([hooks: using .default scaffolds](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/README.md), [frontend guide](../../agent-docs/frontend/README.md)).
- The generator creates hook scaffolds as `.default` files under `src/custom/hooks/` (`pages/`, `dialogs/`, `containers/`, `custom-implementations/`, `components/cards/`, `sub-themes/`). Custom components registered through Pandino (custom visual element implementations) live next to them ([extension points](../../agent-docs/frontend/extension-points.md)).
- Files outside `src/custom/` that the generator owns carry a generated header and are checksum-protected: editing them fails the build unless they are listed in `.generator-ignore`, which also stops generator updates for that file. Keeping hand-written code in a separate directory avoids that trade-off.
- `src/custom/application-customizer.tsx` is a plain generated file with an empty `customize(context)` method; register custom hooks and components from there.
[985] TOOLRESULT: 
- Hooks register as Pandino services from `src/custom/application-customizer.tsx`, normally by copying the generated `.default` scaffolds under `src/custom/hooks/` to names without `.default` and calling their `register...(context)` functions ([hooks: using .default scaffolds](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/README.md)).

  const LinkComponent: ForwardRefExoticComponent<RefAttributes<HTMLAnchorElement>> = forwardRef((props, ref) => (
    <Link
      {...props}
      to={item.url!}
      ref={ref}
      onClick={(e) => {
        // menu item-based navigations should always clear the breadcrumbs
        e.preventDefault();
        e.stopPropagation();
        if (typeof item.onClick === 'function') {
          try {
            item.onClick();
          } catch (e) {
            console.error(e);
          }
        } else if (item.url!.startsWith('http')) {
          externalNavigate(item.url!);
        } else {
          clearNavigate(item.url!);
        }
      }}
    />
  ));

  return (
    <>

          title={
            miniDrawer && level !== undefined && level <= 1

              : undefined
          }

```typescript

[1167] TOOLRESULT: 
Instead of matching translated menu text, use the `href` attribute:

```typescript
// BAD - Depends on locale
await page.getByRole("link", { name: "Administration" }).click();
await page.getByRole("link", { name: "ADMINISZTRÁCIÓ" }).click();

// GOOD - Language-independent href pattern
const adminLink = page.locator('a[href*="/Service/Actor/Administration/"]');
await adminLink.click();
```

**href patterns by menu type**:
- Administration: `/Service/Actor/Administration/`
- Dashboard: `/Service/Actor/Dashboard/`
- Access pages: `/Service/Actor/{PageName}/`

[1206] ASSISTANT: ## Round 1 report: price display (S04 redone, S09)

I built the price display for the form and the details view, but I could not check it. The list column is skipped. The menu would not open in the browser, so I never saw any of these screens.

### What I built (committed locally)
- **S04 revert:** I restored the generated `.gitignore` and `.generator-ignore` from commit `b1c6f91`, removed the patched table component from git, and regenerated. The table component is generated again and has no `minimumFractionDigits`.
- **Shared price component:** `src/custom/components/Price.tsx` contains `formatPrice` (gives `79.90 EUR`), `Price` (read-only) and `PriceField` (the editable field). `PriceField` shows `79.90 EUR` when the field is not focused and a plain number with an EUR suffix while you type.
- **Form and details view:** the model flag `customImplementation=true` is set on `ProductInfo_Form::price` and `ProductInfo_View_Edit::price`. I used the two scaffolds the generator produced under `src/custom/hooks/custom-implementations/register…CustomImplementations.tsx`. Both use `PriceField`.
- **Registration:** the two register calls are in `src/custom/application-customizer.tsx`, and that file is now listed in the module `.generator-ignore`. This is an exception to the "no `.generator-ignore`" rule (see issue 3).
- **Table column: skipped.** No hook can do it (issue 1).

### Catalog paths used
- `agent-docs/frontend/hooks/table-hooks.md` and `ui-hooks.md`
- `agent-docs/frontend/extension-points.md`

### Issues (all logged in GAPS.md under R1-1 to R1-5 and R1-T)
1. **Column hook cannot be used. Catalog gap plus generator bug.**
   - **Docs:** `table-hooks.md` documents `TABLE_COLUMN_CUSTOMIZER_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` but never says the column needs `customImplementation=true`. I found that by trying it.
   - **Generator bug:** with the flag on, the generated table fails to compile. `useTrackService` is not imported, and the filter string is missing a closing parenthesis. The kb's "Frontend Build Recovery" did not help.
   - **What I did:** I did not patch the file. I turned the flag back off, so the list still shows `79.90` as the generated code formats it. I did not confirm this on screen.
   - **Signature:** the doc example is `(original) => …`, but the shipped type is `() => (original) => GridColDef`. The shipped type is moot because the hook cannot be used.
2. **No "view field hook" exists. Catalog gap.** `ui-hooks.md` covers only the AppBar, hero, logo, footer and menu. The only route is a model-level custom implementation on the field. Nothing in the kb says it works on a plain DataField.
3. **Registration needs an edit to a generated file.**
   - **What and why:** the only documented place is `application-customizer.tsx`, and editing it needs a `.generator-ignore` entry. I asked you through `ask_user`, you answered Yes, and then you told me never to use it. That was my mistake, and I won't use it again.
   - **Where logged:** GAPS.md R1-3 records the exception.
4. **My own mistakes.**
   - **Fetch order:** I ran the model change while the CLI jar was missing, and it silently did not apply. The working order is: fetch the jar, change the model, save, `generate -i`, `build -f -i`.
   - **Typing:** I mis-typed the Keycloak password once and got "Invalid username or password".
5. **Catalog gaps in the build process.**
   - **Missing jar:** `./judo.sh generate` deletes `target/judo-cli.jar`

Your menu-click problem is the browser TOOL, not the app. I tested it: the agent-browser tool delivers no click events to the page at all. A capture listener on `document` saw zero clicks for ref, text and raw mouse clicks. `set viewport` lags, and `open` sometimes leaves the tab on `about:blank`. Log this in GAPS.md as a tool issue, and stop using the `browser` tool for checks.

From now on, every browser check is a Playwright E2E test, the JUDO way. Before you start, read the catalog with kb_search/kb_get: agent-docs/e2e-testing (README.md, writing-tests.md, helpers-reference.md, language-independent-testing.md, troubleshooting.md).

This round, ONE feature: the E2E check setup, plus a real check of S04 and S09.
1. Set up the E2E module or project as the catalog describes. Find in the kb how the ESM template generates it. If the template has no E2E module, make a minimal Playwright project in `e2e/` and log that gap.

   - Check that the 3 products and their prices are shown.

   - Back returns to the list.

## Project Structure

```
application/e2e/\<app>__[actor_fqn]/
├── playwright/
│   ├── tests/                      # ✏️ TEST FILES HERE
│   │   └── *.spec.ts               # Playwright test files
│   ├── helpers/                    # 📦 GENERATED UTILITIES
│   │   ├── ApiHelper.ts            # API request handling

import { test as setup, expect } from "@playwright/test";
import path from "path";

const authFile = path.join(__dirname, "../.auth/user.json");

setup("authenticate", async ({ page }) => {
  // Go to the app - will redirect to Keycloak login
  await page.goto("/");

  // Wait for Keycloak login page using language-independent id selector
  await expect(page.locator("#kc-form-login")).toBeVisible({ timeout: 10000 });

  // Fill in credentials using Keycloak's standard input ids (language-independent)
  await page.locator("#username").fill("admin");
  await page.locator("#password").fill("admin");

  // Click sign in using Keycloak's standard button id (language-independent)
  await page.locator("#kc-login").click();

  // Wait for redirect back to app
  await expect(page).toHaveTitle(/\<app>/i, { timeout: 30000 });

  // Save authentication state for other tests
  await page.context().storageState({ path: authFile });
});

[1239] TOOLRESULT: 
To enable the E2E generator in JUDO, edit the `generator-parameter.properties` file in your project root and change:

```properties
generateE2eModule=false
```

to:

```properties
generateE2eModule=true
```

Then rebuild the project with:

```bash
./judo.sh build
```

This will generate an E2E test module in your application structure. The E2E tests are typically generated using Playwright for React frontends.

[1253] TOOLRESULT: ## Test File Structure

Tests are organized in `playwright/tests/` directory. Each test file should focus on a specific feature or page.

### Basic Test Structure

```typescript
import { test, expect, Page } from '@playwright/test';
import { TableHelper } from '../helpers/TableHelper';
import { ApiHelper } from '../helpers/ApiHelper';
import { 
  navigateToAccess,
  fillPrimitiveField,
  TEST_DATA_PREFIX 
} from '../helpers/utils';
import { faker } from '@faker-js/faker';

test.describe('Feature Name', () => {
  const tableHelper = new TableHelper();
  const apiHelper = new ApiHelper();

  // Optional: Shared setup function
  const prepareTest = async (page: Page, data: string): Promise<void> => {
    await apiHelper.gotoWithWait(page);
    // ... setup steps
  };

  test('Test case description', async ({ page }) => {
    // Arrange
    const testData = TEST_DATA_PREFIX + faker.string.alpha(10);
    
    // Act
    await prepareTest(page, testData);
    
    // Assert
    await expect(page.getByText(testData)).toBeVisible();
  });
});
```

## Navigation Patterns

### Navigate to Dashboard

```typescript
// Navigate to app root and wait for network idle
await apiHelper.gotoWithWait(page);
```

### Navigate to Access Table (Menu Item)

```typescript
// Click menu button and wait for list API response
await navigateToAccess(page, '󰵲 Galaxies');

// With custom regex pattern
await navigateToAccess(page, '󰵲 Entities', /.*~list$/);
```

### Navigate to View Page

```typescript
// C
[1254] TOOLRESULT: // Fill form
await fillPrimitiveField(page, "Name *", entityName);

// Submit and wait for list refresh
await submitOnAccesListCreate(page);
```

### Update Entity

```typescript
import { updateAccessViewPage } from '../helpers/utils';

// Make changes to form fields
await fillPrimitiveField(page, "Name", "Updated Name");

// Save changes
await updateAccessViewPage(page);
```

### Delete Entity

```typescript
// Click delete button
await page.getByRole('button', { name: '󰗨 Delete' }).click();

// Confirm deletion
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Yes' }).click();
```

## Table Interactions

### Get Table Rows

```typescript
const tableHelper = new TableHelper();

// Get specific row (0 is the header row, so the first data row is 1)
const row = tableHelper.getRow(page, 1);

// Get all data rows (excludes header)
const rows = tableHelper.getRows(page);

// Count rows
await expect(tableHelper.getRows(page)).toHaveCount(5);
```

### Get Cell Content

```typescript
// Get cell by column data-field
const cell = tableHelper.getCell(tableHelper.getRow(page, 1), "name");

// Assert cell content
await expect(cell).toContainText("Expected Value");
await expect(cell).toHaveText("Exact Value");
```

### Assert Multiple Rows

```typescript
import { assertRows, assertRowContent } from '../helpers/utils';

// Assert multiple rows at once
await assertRows(tableHelper, page, [
  [{ cellName: 'name', textOperation: 'Contain', value: 'Entity A' }],
  [{ cellName: 'name', textOperation: 'Co

export class ApiHelper {
    regex : RegExp = new RegExp(".*"+ (process.env.APP_URL || 'http://localhost:8181' ) + "/api/webshop/Shop" + ".*");

    triggerAndWait(page : Page, locator: Locator ) : Promise<any> {
        return Promise.all([page.waitForResponse(this.regex), locator.click()]);
    }

    triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page : Page, locator : Locator, regex : RegExp) : Promise<any> {
        return Promise.all([page.waitForResponse(regex), locator.click()]);
    }

    triggerAndWaitWithAction(page : Page, promise : Promise<void>, regex : RegExp) : Promise<any> {
        return Promise.all([page.waitForResponse(regex), promise]);
    }

    gotoWithWait(page: Page, url: string = ''): Promise<any> {
        return page.goto(url, { waitUntil: 'networkidle' });
    }
}

export async function assertRows(tableHelper: TableHelper, page: Page, rows: Array<CellAssertData[]>) {
    for (let idxR = 0; idxR < rows.length; idxR++) {
        await assertRowContent(tableHelper, page, idxR + 1, rows[idxR]);

}

export async function assertRowContent(tableHelper: TableHelper, page: Page, rowNum: number, data: CellAssertData[]) {
    for (const cell of data) {
        await assertCellContent(tableHelper, page, rowNum, cell);
    }
}

export async function assertCellContent(tableHelper: TableHelper, page: Page, rowNum: number, data: CellAssertData) {
    if (data.textOperation === 'Have') {
        await expect(tableHelper.getCell(tableHelper.getRow(page, rowNum), data.cellName)).toHaveText(data.value);

        await expect(tableHelper.getCell(tableHelper.getRow(page, rowNum), data.cellName)).toContainText(data.value);

            await expect(tableHelper.getCell(tableHelper.getRow(page, rowNum), data.cellName).locator('> div span')).toHaveClass('mdi mdi-check-circle JUDO-mdi-icon true');

// Price field for the form and the details page: shows "79.90 EUR" when not focused, plain number while typing.
export const PriceField: FC<PriceFieldProps> = ({
  data,
  validation,
  editMode,
  isLoading,
  storeDiff,
  required = true,
}) => {
  const { t } = useTranslation();
  const [draft, setDraft] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const error = validation?.get('price');
  const readOnly = !editMode || data?.__updateable === false;

  const shown = draft !== null ? draft : formatPrice(data?.price);

  const onChange = (e: ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement>) => {
    const text = e.target.value.replace(',', '.');
    if (text !== '' && !/^\d*\.?\d{0,2}$/.test(text)) {
      return;
    }
    setDraft(text);
    storeDiff('price', text === '' || text === '.' ? undefined : Number(text));
  };

  return (
    <TextField
      id="price"
      name="price"
      fullWidth
      variant="filled"
      label={t('Product.price', { defaultValue: 'Price' }) as string}
      required={required}
      disabled={isLoading}
      value={shown}
      error={!!error}
      helperText={error}
      onFocus={() => setDraft(data?.pri

---
## Intent

Some values are the same for every row: an application name, a version label, or a boolean that must always be true or false so that a UI control stays permanently enabled or disabled. Expose them as a derived attribute whose getter expression is a literal.

## Practice

### Literal getter on a derived attribute

Declare a derived attribute (`memberType = DERIVED`) with a getter expression that is a constant: a quoted string such as `"Example Shop"`, or `true` / `false`. Nothing is stored and the owner needs no matching

[1414] TOOLRESULT: ## Customizing Theme

> **Prefer the model.** Brand colours belong on the `ActorType` colour attributes (layer 1 above); regenerating then updates the palette. Edit `src/theme/palette.ts` only for values the actor attributes cannot express. Because the generator overwrites `palette.ts`, a hand edit must be listed in `.generator-ignore`, and from then on model colour changes no longer reach it.

### Step 1: Modify Palette File

```bash
cd application/frontend-react/<app>__[actor_fqn]

# Edit theme file
vim src/theme/palette.ts
```

### Step 2: Add to .generator-ignore

```bash
# Protect from regeneration
echo "src/theme/palette.ts" >> .generator-ignore
```

### Step 3: Customize Colors

```typescript
// src/theme/palette.ts
export const paletteThemeLight = createTheme({
  palette: {
    mode: 'light',
    primary: {
      main: '#1976d2',  // Custom primary color
    },
    secondary: {
      main: '#dc004e',  // Custom secondary color
    },
  },
});
```

### Step 4: Test Changes

```bash
# Start dev server
pnpm run dev

# Changes apply immediately via HMR
```

[1415] TOOLRESULT: ## Theme Override Fragments

The generated theme (`src/theme/index.tsx`) is assembled from fixed fragments that you can override by creating a same-named file in the application's `actor/src/theme/` override directory (see [Template Override Process](./README.md#template-override-process)):

- `index.ts.mui-<component>.fragment.hbs` for `app-bar`, `autocomplete`, `avatar`, `button`, `card`, `chip`, `data-grid`, `data-grid-pro`, `dialog`, `divider`, `drawer`, `form-control`, `icon`, `input-base`, `list`, `paper`, `radio`, `select`, `stack`, `tab`, `text-field`, `tooltip`
- `index.ts.extra-components.fragment.hbs` and `index.ts.theme-options.fragment.hbs`
- `palette.ts.extra-imports.fragment.hbs`, `palette.ts.extra-attributes.fragment.hbs`, `palette.ts.extra-palette-attributes.fragment.hbs`

Prefer a fragment override over a full `.generator-ignore` of `index.tsx` when you only change one component's styling.

## Sub-Themes for Components

A sub-theme applies different MUI styling to widgets that have a `subTheme` name set in the UI model. The generated widget is wrapped in `SubThemeWrapper`, which looks up a Pandino service under `SUB_THEME_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` (value `'SubThemeHook'`) with the matching `name` property, calls it with the parent theme and deep-merges the result into the theme for that subtree.

### Configuration

The generator emits a scaffold per sub-theme name: `src/custom/hooks/sub-themes/register<Name>SubThemeHook.tsx.default`. Copy it, remove the `.default`

title: Frontend Theming and Styling

# Theming and Styling

## Overview

The frontend uses Material-UI (MUI) theming system for consistent styling. Themes can be customized through palette configuration and applied globally or to specific components.

JUDO theming is **two-layered**:

1. **Actor palette (model-driven)** — the `ActorType` in the ESM model carries 7 color attributes (`primaryColor`, `secondaryColor`, `backgroundColor`, `paperBackgroundColor`, `textPrimaryColor`, `textSecondaryColor`, `subtitleColor`) plus `applicationLogo`. The generator renders these into `src/theme/palette.ts`. **To change the app-wide brand palette, edit the ActorType in the ESM model — not `palette.ts` directly** (editing `palette.ts` requires a `.generator-ignore` entry and loses cross-actor consistency).
2. **Component sub-themes (frontend)** — a `subTheme` name set on a widget in the UI model selects a registered `SubThemeHook` service that returns partial MUI overrides, applied via a nested `ThemeProvider`. Use this for per-card, per-view, per-table color variations.

For the **authoring side** (ESM mutations to set ActorType colors, plan sub-theme keys via consistent Group naming, and enable data-driven `generateVisualPropertiesHook` / `TableRowHighlightingHook`), see the `judo-model-cli` skill's `ui-mutations.md` — specifically the

## Practice

- Logo and icon defaults come from the actor's `applicationLogo` in the model; prefer changing the model for a permanent change. A `Cus

# Internationalization (i18n)

## Overview

The frontend uses **react-i18next** for internationalization. Translation files are located in the `public/i18n/` directory and support multiple languages.

## Directory Structure

**IMPORTANT**: JUDO generates translation files in a flat structure, NOT in subdirectories.

```
public/i18n/
├── application_default.json   # Generated application translations (fallback)
├── application_en-US.json     # Generated application translations (English)
├── application_hu-HU.json     # Generated application translations (Hungarian)
├── system_default.json        # JUDO system translations (fallback)
├── system_en-US.json          # JUDO system translations (English)
└── system_hu-HU.json          # JUDO system translations (Hungarian)
```

**Key Points**:
- ❌ **NOT** organized in subdirectories like `en-US/translation.json`
- ✅ **YES** flat structure with `{type}_{locale}.json` naming
- **application_*.json** - Generated from your model (entities, enumerations, menu, services)
- **system_*.json** - JUDO platform UI translations (buttons, modals, table controls)
- **_default.json** files serve as fallback when specific locale not found

## Translation File Format

### Structure

**JUDO uses flat dot-notation keys**, not nested objects:

```json

[1454] TOOLRESULT: fragment: "none: frontend-only"
first_seen: 2026-03-04
last_updated: 2026-11-10
---
## Intent

Custom UI code (hand-written components, custom operations, project fault messages) needs translation keys that the model does not produce. This practice gives those keys English defaults in the generated `application_default.json` without editing the generated file, so they survive regeneration.

## Practice

### Platform base

- `public/i18n/application_*.json` files are generated from the model and are overwritten on regeneration (or fail the checksum check) unless listed in `.generator-ignore`; keys follow flat dot notation inside a `translation` object ([i18n](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md)).
- The generator supports fragment overrides: a file with the same relative path and name as a template's fragment, placed in the override directory, is picked up on every generation and needs no `.generator-ignore` entry ([fragment overrides](../../agent-docs/deployment/build-process.md)).
- The platform key patterns include `extra.localization.operation.<operationName>` for custom operation labels and `faults.<app>...` for fault messages ([i18n](../../agent-docs/frontend/i18n.md)).

### Project practice: a fragment for `application_default.json`

The agent-docs describe fragment overrides for `pom.xml` and `feature.xml`; the i18n slot below is project practice (rackinspect) and is not covered by the agent-docs, so check the generated output after adding it.

- Place `application_defau

[1460] TOOLRESULT: 
Some generated files (mostly `pom.xml` and `feature.xml`) cannot be fully controlled by template parameters because they need to carry project-specific content (extra dependencies, plugins, bundles, …). For these, the templates expose **named fragment extension points** that you can fill in without touching the generated output.

**How to spot one:** generated files contain paired marker comments like

```xml
<!-- To define create 'app/pom.xml.extra-dependencies.fragment.hbs' file -->
<!-- End of 'app/pom.xml.extra-dependencies.fragment.hbs' -->
```

By default the fragment resolves to an empty stub from the template jar. You override it by dropping a file with the **same relative path and name** into the override directory.

**Override directory:** `application/generator-overrides/` (wired into the generator invocation in the root `pom.xml` via `<uri>${maven.multiModuleProjectDirectory}/application/generator-overrides</uri>`; a second override root `generator-overrides/` exists for the project-root generator step).

Root cause (platform bug): in judo-ng's ESM→UI transformation, `judo-tatami-esm2ui/src/main/epsilon/transformations/ui/modules/ui/dataField.etl` has one ETL rule per widget type. So inside one container, number fields always come before text fields; the model order and `row` are ignored. Log this in GAPS.md as a platform bug with that reference, replacing R3-1.

[1685] USER: ROUND 5. Round 4 is closed. Field order is a platform bug, and it is not your fault. Update GAPS.md R3-1 with the real cause:
- `esmToUi.etl` sorts container children by ESM position (pre block around line 77, post block line 114).
- But `operations/ui/visualElement.eol:5` looks the position up with `__pos.get(component) ?: 0`. For these fields that lookup misses, so all positions are 0, and the stable sort keeps the rule-firing order: number widgets first.
- Groups are created lazily from their first child's rule, so they follow the same order.

Remove the three wrapper groups again, because they add nothing. Mark the two field-order tests with `test.fail()` and a comment that names the bug, so the suite is green with a known failure.

[1689] TOOLRESULT: # Frontend Extension Points

Supported extension points of the generated React frontend that are not hooks of a specific page.

## Extra Routes

`src/extra-routes.tsx` exports `extraRoutes: Array<{ path: string; element: ReactElement; exact?: boolean }>`. It is generated empty, and the generated `routes.tsx` prepends these routes to the generated ones. Add custom React Router routes there instead of editing generated routing files (protect the file in `.generator-ignore`).

## Guest Page

With `supportGuestAccess` enabled on the application, an unauthenticated user is not redirected to `signinRedirect`. The auth gate renders a guest page instead. Register your own component as a service under `GUEST_PAGE_INTERFACE_KEY` (the value `'GuestPage'`, imported from `~/auth/Auth`):

```typescript
context.registerService<FC<any>>(GUEST_PAGE_INTERFACE_KEY, GuestPage);
```

The scaffold `src/custom/hooks/default-guest-page-hook-registration.tsx.default` contains `registerGuestPage(context)`. The default page shows a Login / Register button (i18n key `judo.guest-page.login-register`) that calls `signinRedirect()`.

## Sub-Theme Hooks

A named sub-theme is registered by `register<SubTheme>SubThemeHook(context)` (scaffold `src/custom/hooks/sub-themes/register<Name>SubThemeHook.tsx.default`):

```typescript
context.registerService<SubThemeHook>(SUB_THEME_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY, mySubTheme, { name: '<safeCamelCaseName>' });
```

`SUB_THEME_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` and `SubThemeHook` come from `~/them

## Intent

A `MenuItemAccess` points at a dashboard transfer object, but the UI model gives that dashboard no layout (no view), so the generator produces no page component for it. The route still needs something to render. A small page component built on `ComponentProxy` fills the gap: it shows a minimal heading until a custom implementation is registered in the Pandino service registry, and it renders that implementation afterwards. Routing is decoupled from the dashboard content, which can arrive in a later change.

## Practice

### Platform base

- Custom UI is added as a Pandino service. A custom implementation of a model element is registered under `CUSTOM_VISUAL_ELEMENT_INTERFACE_KEY` (from `~/custom`) with the service property `component` set to a per-element key, and the register function is called from `application-customizer.tsx` ([custom component replacement](../../agent-docs/frontend/advanced-patterns.md), [application customizer hub](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/AGENTS.md)).
- Files under `src/custom/` are never regenerated ([action hooks, keep custom hooks under src/custom](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/action-hooks.md)).
- Whether the generator really emits no page for a dashboard without a layout, and the exact `ComponentProxy` props, are *verify* items: check the generated sources of your version.

### Project practice: placeholder page per dashboard (compsychletter)

## Intent

An application with several roles has one home route, but not every role may open the page behind it. The generated page for a role that is not allowed to see it fails to load and the app then shows the not-found page. A small component mounted as the access filter looks at the signed-in principal and the menu the principal can see, and moves roles that cannot use the home page to their first visible menu entry.

## Practice

### Platform base

- Register one service under `ACCESS_FILTER_COMPONENT_INTERFACE_KEY` from `application-customizer.tsx`. It receives `{ principal, children }`, is rendered around the authenticated layout, and without a registered service the children render unchanged ([access filtering](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/navigation-and-access.md#access-filtering)).
- Only one filter is used, and it is not selected per page, so page-level rules are decided inside the component, for example with `useLocation()` (same section).
- Visibility of menu entries is modeled with `hiddenBy` on the actor accesses, so the backend decides what each role sees ([access flags](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/navigation-and-access.md)).
- A client-side filter is a UX layer only. Real authorization stays in the backend.

### Project practice: the landing redirect

This layer is project practice (rackinspect), not a platform feature:

- **Derive, do not map.** Read the generated me

## Intent

Part of a page needs UI the generator cannot produce (charts, masked inputs, rich viewers). Model that part as a custom implementation element and supply the React component yourself; the rest of the page stays generated.

## Practice

### Platform base

- A custom implementation is a visual element of the ESM UI model that the generator exposes as an extension point. It is not a way to swap an arbitrary generated component: the element must be modeled first ([widgets: placeholder / custom implementation](../../agent-docs/frontend/esm-to-ui-mappings/widgets.md), [advanced patterns: custom component replacement](../../agent-docs/frontend/advanced-patterns.md)).
- You register a function component as a Pandino service under `CUSTOM_VISUAL_ELEMENT_INTERFACE_KEY` (exported from `~/custom`) with the service property `component` set to the per-element key generated in the container's `customization.ts`. There is no generic `CUSTOM_COMPONENT_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY`.
- For containers that have such elements the generator creates the scaffold `src/custom/hooks/custom-implementations/register<Container>CustomImplementations.tsx.default` with one `register<Interface>CustomImplementation(context)` function per element and the right props type (`GenericProxyProps`, `TableProxyProps`, `LinkProxyProps` from `~/custom`). Copy it without `.default`, implement the component and call the register function

[1696] TOOLRESULT: #### Priority-Based Highlighting

```typescript
const priorityHighlightHook: TableRowHighlightingHook<IssueStored> = () => () => [
  { name: 'critical', label: 'Critical', backgroundColor: '#ffebee', fontColor: '#d32f2f', condition: ({ row }) => row.priority === 'CRITICAL' },
  { name: 'high', label: 'High', backgroundColor: '#fff3e0', condition: ({ row }) => row.priority === 'HIGH' },
];
```

#### Threshold-Based Highlighting

```typescript
const inventoryHighlightHook: TableRowHighlightingHook<ProductStored> = () => () => [
  { name: 'out-of-stock', label: 'Out of stock', backgroundColor: '#ffebee', fontColor: '#c62828', condition: ({ row }) => row.currentStock === 0 },
  { name: 'low-stock', label: 'Low stock', backgroundColor: '#fff3e0', fontColor: '#e65100', condition: ({ row }) => row.currentStock > 0 && row.currentStock / row.maxStock <= 0.2 },
];
```

Stylers are evaluated per row; define the most specific conditions so only one applies to a row.

---

## Custom Component Replacement

### Overview

Replacement of model-designated UI elements with custom implementations while maintaining JUDO integration.

### Use Cases

- Complex custom layouts
- Third-party component integration
- Specialized visualizations
- Custom interaction patterns

// One price format for the whole shop: two decimals plus currency, e.g. "79.90 EUR".
export const PRICE_CURRENCY = 'EUR';

export function formatPrice(value?: number | string | null): string {
  if (value === null || value === undefined || value === '') {
    return '';
  }
  const n = typeof value === 'number' ? value : Number(value);
  if (Number.isNaN(n)) {
    return '';
  }
  return `${n.toFixed(2)} ${PRICE_CURRENCY}`;
}

// Read-only display (used by cards and any custom page).

  <span data-testid="price">{formatPrice(value)}</span>
);

[1742] ASSISTANT: ## Round 5 report: S08 catalog home and S10 details layout

The full suite is green: 17 passed and 1 skipped, in one run at the end. The skipped test is the empty-state check. Everything is committed locally as `b7cfad3`.

### What I built
- **GAPS.md R3-1:** replaced with the real cause you gave (`esmToUi.etl` sorting, the `visualElement.eol:5` lookup, the per-widget rule order, lazy groups).
- **Wrapper groups:** removed again from the form and the view.
- **S08, card grid as home:**
  - **Component:** `CatalogHome.tsx`. Each card has an image placeholder (a gradient with a mountain SVG), the name, the price through the shared `Price` component, and the first line of the description.
  - **Responsive:** 1 column at 375, 2 at 768, 3 or 4 at 1440.
  - **Data and navigation:** it uses the generated `ShopServiceForProductsImpl.list(...)` and the app's own navigation (`useJudoNavigation` and `routeToShopProductsAccessViewPage`), so Back returns to the home.
  - **States:** loading skeletons, an empty state, and an error alert with a Retry button.
  - **Extension point:** it is registered through the generated dashboard page's own seam (see issue 1).
- **S10, details layout:** `ProductDetailsLayout.tsx` shows a large name, then a price badge using the shared `PriceField`, then a multi-line description. Edit still works, because every change goes through `storeDiff` and the generated page then shows Update and Cancel.
- **Tests:**
  - **S08:** the grid shows 3 cards with name, price and a one-line description; the column counts at 375, 768 and 1440; a card click opens the details page; Back returns to the grid.
  - **S10:** the order Name, Price, Description top to bottom, a name font size of at least 28 px, and an edit, save and restore round trip.
  - **Existing tests:** the earlier tests now wait for the card grid instead of "Hello!".

### Catalog paths used
- `agent-docs/frontend/extension-points.md`, `advanced-patterns.md` (custom component replacement), `hooks/data-hooks.md`, `esm-to-ui-mappings/widgets.md`
- `best-practices/frontend/custom-visual-element-override.md`, `component-proxy-placeholder-page.md`, `role-landing-redirect-access-filter.md`
- `agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/ui.md`

### Issues
1. **The home seam is not in the kb. Catalog gap, minor.**
   - **What exists:** the generated `DashboardPage` already renders a `ComponentProxy` for the custom-element key `DashboardPage`. `<h1>Hello!</h1>` is only its fallback.
   - **What I did:** I registered a service under that key. No generated file was touched and no extra route was needed.
   - **What the kb says:** it only describes this pattern for a dashboard without a layout, and Extra Routes. I found the seam by reading the generated code.
2. **No documented hook for a whole view layout. Catalog gap (GAPS R5-2).**
   - **Placeholder fails:** a `ui:Placeholder` with `customImplementation` generates no scaffold and renders nothing.
   - **Missing data:** I first removed the price and description fields and kept only a custom `name`. The details page then showed empty price and description. The generated view only loads attributes that have a field in its query.
   - **Final shape:** all three view fields are custom implementations. Only `name` renders, and it renders the whole layout. `price` and `description` register a component that renders nothing.
   - **Cost:** three register functions for one layout, and the view depends on those field names.
3. **One deviation from your round text.**
   - **Marked failing:** I marked only the create-form order test `test.fail()`, with a comment naming the bug.
   - **Why only one:** the details order test now passes, because the custom layout fixes the order. `test.fail()` on a passing test would turn the suite red.
   - **Remaining effect:** the generated create dialog still shows Price first.
4. **Empty state is not tested (GAPS R5-4).** The kb documents no empty-data route, and the app has no switch for it. The empty state i
